## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0883]

Kind: company_relationship · Source: full:b22b5394af642e94d5e623a2

Fielding identifies Switch Mobile as her company and characterizes its outcome profile as between a slow-and-steady business and a two-to-three-times return.

> I'd say my company Switch Mobile was maybe the mix between a slow and steady and a double and a triple, right?

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0884]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:b22b5394af642e94d5e623a2

Fielding reports having invested in approximately 130 companies.

> I've invested in about 130 companies and these things come up time and time again of are you pitching the right deal to the right investor?

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0885]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:b22b5394af642e94d5e623a2

Fielding describes Techstars as taking common stock to align its interests with founders, without specifying her formal role there.

> So at Techstars, you know, we feel very aligned with our founders because we actually take common stock.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0886]

Kind: biography · Source: full:b22b5394af642e94d5e623a2

Fielding says she lived in New York and describes sub-$1 million notes or SAFEs as common and easy in that market.

> That said, the general rule is, at least in the U.S. and New York where I live, under a million dollars, super easy to do a note or a safe.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0887]

Kind: dated_event · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said the fund began in 2018 after its organizers started considering it in 2017.

> But we started this in 2018. We started thinking about 2017 was like a little bit unconventional.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0888]

Kind: company_relationship · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding described Everywhere as having 500 founder and operator LPs distributed globally.

> we've built, you know, this group of 500 founder and operator LPs. They're located around the world.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0889]

Kind: biography · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said she has built and invested in companies since 2007.

> Well, I mean, I've been building companies and investing companies now since 2007.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0890]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding characterized Everywhere's model as a traditional fund operating like a decentralized angel network under a community-powered venture-capital approach.

> We kind of always called it community -powered venture capital. So we have a traditional fund, but we kind of operate like a decentralized angel network.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0891]

Kind: company_relationship · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said Everywhere had 250 companies and used scalable systems rather than twice-weekly calls with every founder.

> in other words, you know, we have 250 companies. We're not, you know, having calls with our founders twice a week. Right. Because that's not what we are capable of doing. So when you do venture at scale, you have to build, you know, scalable systems.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0892]

Kind: company_relationship · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said Latch entered her program as two founders with a prototype and later became public.

> like one of the coolest companies was a company that, you know, was two guys and a prototype when they entered my program. And that company actually went public, a company called Latch.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0893]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said nontechnical founders must learn technical operations while technical founders may need to learn sales leadership.

> And I think you have a lot of founders maybe that aren't technical or the technical ones have to understand how to run a sales team.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0894]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding qualified her emphasis on persistence by saying founders also need the skill to recognize when to cut their losses.

> And so, you know, although like knowing when to cut your losses is definitely a skill and

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0895]

Kind: company_relationship · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding described a founder she was advising as having 18 months of runway.

> he's got, uh, 18 months of runway, so plenty of runway.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0896]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said she and her co-founder planned to continue raising Everywhere funds for many years.

> So my co -founder and I plan to be raising funds from now for, for many years.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0897]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said Everywhere is generally the first check and invests up to $250,000 at very early stages.

> you know, we're just the first check, right? So we write up to 250 K into these companies all at those really early stages.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0898]

Kind: dated_event · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said Everywhere was in the early stages of considering an opportunity fund for follow-on support.

> And so I think, you know, that's kind of the thing that we're thinking about is like, what does, you know, opportunity fund look like for us?

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0899]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding contrasted Everywhere's intended strategy with funds that move away from pre-seed and seed after raising $100 million vehicles.

> And we've seen so many funds that start off doing pre -seed and seed, then they raise these hundred million dollar funds.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0900]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:591d70178cd086db4b966c69

Fielding said Everywhere intends to remain small and retain its mission of supporting founders at the earliest stage of their journey.

> So we want to stay small. Um, and we want to ultimately, you know, be supporting the founders, you know, through the mission that we started with, which is, um, you know, the earliest stages of founders journey.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0901]

Kind: biography · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says her entrepreneurial family background, including her grandfather's Brooklyn fruit stores, influenced her perspective on business-building and resilience.

> My grandfather owned fruit stores throughout Brooklyn and just seeing kind of the resilience that you need to build a business and, you know, just the lifestyle that they had, but then also kind of the freedom that they had was very inspirational for me.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0902]

Kind: biography · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says she attended law school and worked in finance before becoming a founder.

> I very traditionally went to law school, I worked in finance. And then when I finally had the guts to start a company, I think I reflected back on just that kind of that nature of, you know, many people in my family who had decided to, you know, become entrepreneurs, maybe sometimes out of necessity.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0903]

Kind: company_relationship · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says she started her first company in 2007 with Danish co-founders and a Denmark-based technical team.

> when I started my first company in 2007, my co -founders were from Denmark. And we had an office in Denmark. We had our tech team in Denmark. And, you I spent half my time there.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0904]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says her Techstars experience taught her how a mentor-supported community model could enable investing in many startups quickly.

> I learned a lot when I was working at Techstars on, you know, how you can invest, you know, quite quickly into a lot of startups using this community model of support, right, and mentors.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0905]

Kind: biography · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says she initially operated two funds during nights and weekends while retaining a full-time job.

> So really ran two funds while I had a full time job, nights and weekends, and kind of took a lot of the learnings over, you know, my entrepreneurial career and my investing career in, you know, and put them into action.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0906]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says Everywhere's first fund assembled 50 founder LPs, many of whom were angel investors with operating knowledge and deal flow.

> we rallied 50 LPs that were all founders who had built companies. Many of them were angel investors and they got a lot of deal flow. They had a lot of knowledge about running companies and some of them had a lot of time to dedicate to us.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0907]

Kind: dated_event · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says the first fund invested in 50 New York companies over roughly two and a half years.

> So it took us about a little over two years, two and a half years to invest that fund. We invested in 50 companies that were all building in New York

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0908]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding reports that Everywhere's community had exchanged more than one million Slack messages focused on founder resources.

> We have a very engaged community. exchanged over one million Slack messages and no cat memes, like all founder resources.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0909]

Kind: dated_event · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says she traveled to Saudi Arabia in early 2024 and that it was likely the final country added to her book.

> took that trip actually early 2024. I think it was the last country that made it into the book.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0910]

Kind: biography · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says that during her Techstars work she developed experience recruiting founders from global markets into her New York program.

> So one of the things that I got really good at was recruiting founders in global markets to come to New York to my program.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0911]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding reports that approximately 70 percent of Everywhere's investments are in North American companies and 30 percent are distributed internationally.

> although 70 % of our investments are actually in companies in North America, that 30 % are all over the world.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0912]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:a5c1aba381387c1f2b0357a8

Fielding says Everywhere was on its third fund and had raised primarily from founders and operators, along with some family offices.

> Fund three. And yeah, we're always, you know, talking to interesting people raised mostly from founders and operators and some family offices that, you know, are excited about our deal flow.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0913]

Kind: biography · Source: full:1d022940162fe938670588b3

In this first-person introduction, Fielding says she started BBC Worldwide Labs and that it helped companies pursue commercial deals with the BBC.

> Jenny from BBC. I started BBC Worldwide Labs, and we focus on helping companies get commercial deals with the BBC.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0914]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:1d022940162fe938670588b3

Fielding says the accelerator colocated startups with BBC business units to reduce the distance between them.

> We put our accelerator in our offices in the midst of our business units, because we feel that part of the disconnect is really the distance between the business units and the startups.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-0915]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:1d022940162fe938670588b3

Fielding says BBC Worldwide Labs did not invest in participating startups, while her separate Digital Ventures group evaluated early-stage companies.

> We're, we're somewhat unique in that we actually don't put money into, um, startups in labs. Uh, but I run a separate group, um, called Digital Ventures where we look at early stage companies. So we kind of keep them separate.

The publisher says the discussion covered Fielding's strategy, the firm's decision not to use follow-on capital, and its navigation of seed and AI markets.

> We talked about Jenny’s strategy, why venture doesn’t scale, why everywhere doesn't use follow-on capital in its funds, and how Everywhere is navigating the Seed and AI markets.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-1032]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:1bcd4c5328cbcce1f33a5202

The episode's takeaways characterize pre-seed investing as important for helping companies reach product-market fit.

> Pre-seed investment is crucial for helping companies find product-market fit.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-1033]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:1bcd4c5328cbcce1f33a5202

The episode's takeaways say that a founder-and-operator community strengthens deal flow.

> Building a strong community of founders and operators enhances deal flow.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-1034]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:1bcd4c5328cbcce1f33a5202

The episode's takeaways report a people-focused approach despite rising AI valuations.

> AI valuations are rising, but we focus on the people behind the companies.

## [ctx:jenny-fielding-everywhere-ventures-1035]

Kind: investment_approach · Source: full:1bcd4c5328cbcce1f33a5202

The episode's takeaways report that venture-firm scaling need not mean a larger fund and emphasize quality.

> Scaling a venture firm doesn't always mean increasing fund size; quality matters more.
